# 2,464 files, 898 MB, that nothing in the game reads

Nothing here changes what a player sees. This is every file left in the repository that nothing reads, with the proof for each. 1,583 files (872 MB) can be deleted now: 857 data files (672 MB), 555 reports and research files (199 MB, mostly screenshots), 165 scripts and 6 tests. The old engine folders add 881 files (25.7 MB) that can go once eight files stop importing them (seven in the new engine and one data table file). The one choice for the owner: whether any screenshot in section 6a is worth keeping. The default is delete. This report deletes nothing; the trim session does.

## What the trim should do first

1. **Delete the screenshots and run output in `docs/reports` that no file names (section 6a, 311 files, 178 MB).** They are evidence from played checks. A PR body on GitHub may link one; this search cannot see those links, so the paths are in section 6a if the owner wants any kept. This is the largest cut that touches no code.
2. **Delete the raw source files nothing live reads (section 5d, 366 files, 523 MB).** The compiled tables the engines read are separate files (for example `src/core2/reference/place-county-relations.generated.ts` holds the compiled rows of the 156 MB `place-county-relations` folder, and no exporter script for it exists on main). Keep any folder you still want a regeneration path for.
3. **Delete the research JSON no code reads (sections 5a and 5b, 450 files, 148 MB).** Section 5b files are read only by old core code or by scripts in section 3, so they go with them.
4. **Delete the scripts in section 3, tier A first (68 files), then tier C together with its test (each row names it).**
5. **Cut the eight engine and table imports of the old folders (section 1), then delete section 2 whole.** Port only what play needs, as `core2-porting-plan.md` already says.

## What was already known and is not repeated

- `docs/reports/duplicates-on-main-2026-10-28.md` rows W9, W10, W11, W14 and W17 name English banks and `data/content` files that only grading tools read. They stay out of the tables here; the trim handles them from that report.
- `docs/reports/dead-code-2026-10-14-keeps.txt` lists the old-engine importers as of October 14. Section 1 is the live version of that list: 55 importers today, the same 55 entries as `scripts/core2-guard/old-imports-baseline.json`.
- `docs/reports/engine-map-2026-10-28.md` section 1 names the engine files; none of them is in the tables below.
- `tests/guards/research-data-has-reader.test.ts` already lists 354 research files found unread on October 16; most of them are in section 5a.

## How each row was decided

A file is listed when every search below found no reader. A reader is a code file, a workflow, `package.json` or a skill that names the file (an import, a path it opens, or its name in a command), or a script that opens its folder. A citation is not a reader: a `stopgaps.json` source field, a generated file's header comment, `.prettierignore`, `CODEOWNERS`, a PR body or another report.

- **Old folders (sections 1 and 2):** the import graph of `scripts/core2-guard/old-imports.mjs`, rebuilt with the same rules. The eight engine and table files are the only non-script, non-test importers.
- **Scripts (section 3):** every tracked file under `scripts/` except tests and the type stubs next to live `.mjs` files. A script is live when a file that is itself live names it or imports it; this is repeated to a fixed point, so a chain of scripts that only name each other is dead together.
- **Data and reports (sections 5 and 6):** the file's name, together with its parent folder name or a path to it, searched in every tracked text file outside `data/`. For `data/research` JSON the guard's own scanner (`scripts/audit/readers.ts`) gives the first answer. Each listed row was then checked a second time with `git grep` for its bare file name across the repository; a row with a reader found that way was dropped (about 60 rows) and a row with only doc mentions was moved to the "only documents" tier.

## What is not listed, and why

- **Files in a folder that a live script names (134 raw source files, 128 MB, in `data/source`; 663 files, 81 MB, in `docs/reports` and `docs/research`).** The script may read each file or only write into the folder, and a name search cannot tell. The trim should check these folder by folder; the guard below makes it a per-file question from now on.
- **Everything in `src/core2/data` (193 files).** Every one is named by an engine, a test or the calibration guard. Four are named only by data files or tests: `employer-callback-gaps.json` and `opening-employer-cash-reference.json` are imported by one test each, `opening-payroll-frequency-reference.json` is hashed only by `src/core2/tooling/calibration-targets.json`, and `retirement-opening-2021.json` is named only by `parameters.json` and `opening-income-funding.json` (a source field). The last two are candidates the trim should check; I did not list them.
- **Engine, view and guard code under `src/` other than the old folders.** Out of scope for this item; the duplicates report and the engine map cover it.
- **Reports and screenshots that a GitHub PR body or an owner message links.** Those links are outside the repository and invisible to this search. If the owner wants any kept, they are named by path in section 6a.
- **A script's one-time exporters.** The Python `export-*.py` and `mine-*.py` rows in section 3 produced data that is still in the repository. Where a row names its output in the proof, delete the exporter only if you accept losing the regeneration path.

## The numbers that differ from the pool item

- The item says 13 files outside `src/simulation` import it. On main the guard baseline has 55: 7 engine files, 1 data table file, 30 scripts and 17 tests. No count of 13 appears in the engine map, the duplicates report or the pool; only the eight engine and table files feed play.
- The item says a first count found 65 of 384 scripts nothing references. Of 486 non-test files under `scripts/` (538 files in all, the rest tests), 165 have no reader outside docs and tests, and 68 are named nowhere at all (tier A).

## Guard proposal

Extend the existing guard rather than adding a second one (one engine per mechanic).

- **Guard id:** `research-data-has-reader` (`tests/guards/research-data-has-reader.test.ts`), widened from `data/research/*.json` to every file under `data/`, `src/core2/data/` and `scripts/` (non-test). A file passes when a reader exists, or it is on the baseline.
- **Baseline file:** `scripts/audit/carcass-baseline.json`, built from the rows in this report, one path per row. It works the way `research-data-allowlist.json` already does. A path may leave the list and never join it. The total has a ceiling that only drops. A listed path that gains a reader or disappears must be removed in the same change. `research-data-allowlist.json` keeps its `rawSource` and `awaitingReader` entries, which carry reasons.
- **What counts as a reader** (one shared function in `scripts/audit/readers.ts`, replacing `researchReaders`): the three kinds it already counts (an import, a quoted repository path, a quoted folder path), plus `.py` files and workflow YAML as readers, plus a skill or `package.json` command naming a script. Readers do not include `src/simulation`, `src/presentation`, `src/authoring`, generated files, tests, comments, or citations.
- **A blind spot this audit found:** the guard reads only `.ts` and `.js` code, so a research file read only by a Python exporter shows as unread. Seven section 5b rows are read by Python scripts (`export-pension-*.py`, `export-state-reserve-rules.py` and `build-starting-law.py`) and were dropped on the second check; the guard must scan `.py` before it ships.
- **Reports:** `docs/reports` is not covered by the guard. A report belongs in the baseline only if a guard or skill reads it. Screenshots go in the PR body, not the tree; the guard rejects a new `.png` under `docs/reports` that no file names.
- **Scripts:** the same function adds `scripts/**` as a tree whose files must have a reader. `scripts/core2-guard/old-imports-baseline.json` stays the baseline for the old folders.
- **Acceptance when built:** the guard's own test file fails on a new unread file under each tree, and the baseline starts at the full list in this report (2,464 paths) and drops with every trim PR.

## Method and limits

Measured, not inferred, unless a row says otherwise: the old-folder import graph (TypeScript's `preProcessFile` plus quoted relative paths, as in `old-imports.mjs`); the name and folder search over every tracked text file (excluding `data/` JSON and CSV, which cite each other); `scripts/audit/readers.ts` for research JSON; `git grep` as the second check. Names that are common (`index.ts`, `summary.json`) match more than they should, so those rows err toward being kept. This report reads no file from the owner's Drive.

Read: OWNER-DECISIONS (through Oct 28), DECISION-REGISTER, LAW; searched docs/ and the pool for: carcass, dead code, unread data, old-engine removal; the duplicates report, engine map, retroactive look-first audit and dead-code keeps cited above; Drive: no connector.

## 3. Scripts nothing runs

Of 486 non-test files under `scripts/`, 165 have no reader outside docs and tests (a first count by file name alone found 65 of 384; this one follows imports and the fixed point of what live scripts name). Test files are not in this list: a runner globs them (`vite.config.ts` has no include list, so every `*.test.*` runs). Folders a live reader opens whole are kept, for example `scripts/prose-eval/fixtures/grounding/` (read by `scripts/prose-eval/cli.ts`) and `scripts/law-wiring/declared/` (read by `scripts/law-wiring/matrix.ts`). Python `export-*.py` and `mine-*.py` rows are one-time exporters; where one names a data file it is named in the proof, because deleting the exporter and keeping its output loses the way to regenerate it.

`data/` is 1,659 files and 1,100.6 MB; `src/core2/data` is 193 files (23.3 MB) and every file there is named by an engine, a test or the calibration guard (0 rows). The raw source in `data/source` is read by exporter scripts that compile it into `src/core2` tables; rows below are the raw files whose exporter is gone or only old core names them. ## 6. Reports and research nothing reads

`docs/reports` is 610 files (284.8 MB) and `docs/research` is 657 files (17.0 MB). Most of the weight is screenshots from played checks: 257 of the 311 unnamed files in `docs/reports` (section 6a) are images. A file the guard-test or skill names stays. 663 files (81.2 MB) sit in a folder a script names but are not named themselves; they are not listed because a browser script that writes a folder looks identical to one that reads it.
